# QA review charter — B2: does the engine mean what the vocabulary says?

You are a fresh-context reviewer. You have full repo access. Read the
repo yourself; do not trust any summary in this charter over the bytes.

This module validates authored workflow documents against a DECLARED
schema. The declaration language is specified in one file — each
construct with a doc comment stating what it means — and a separate
engine interprets declarations written in it. **This round checks the two
against each other.**

It is the foundation every later phase builds on, which is why its owner
has bought extra rounds for it. Earlier rounds covered accident and
sloppiness in the build, the soundness of the direction, whether the
load-time gate resolves every name, and runtime robustness on unusual
input. **Conformance between the specification and the interpreter has
never been checked.**

## 0. READ THIS FIRST — the time budget

**You have a HARD 20-minute wall-clock budget, enforced by an external
process kill. A session that hits it produces NO usable output — every
finding you have not yet written out is discarded.** An earlier session in
this repo spent its budget investigating, planned to write up at the end,
and was killed mid-write-up; all of its work was lost.

**Write each finding out IN FULL, in the §4 format, the moment you
confirm it.** Stop investigating around the 12-minute mark. Breadth at
shallow depth; anything costing over two minutes goes down as `unrun`
with one sentence on what would settle it.

## 1. The review object

| File | Lines | sha256 (first 16) |
|---|---|---|
| `v3/src/definition/schema/vocabulary.ts` | 436 | `890f458676afffa7` | the SPECIFICATION |
| `v3/src/definition/schema/engine.ts` | 1123 | `b753f4fc58ce9f81` | the INTERPRETER |
| `v3/src/definition/schema/normalizer.ts` | 140 | `30754f4b7aa93199` |
| `v3/src/definition/schema/templateFormat.ts` | 574 | `69719e9fc9196e14` | one declaration written in it |

`vocabulary.ts` is the language specification: every construct carries a
doc comment stating its meaning, and several state a rule explicitly (a
grain, an ordering, a scope, a default). `engine.ts` interprets it.
`defineSurface.ts` gates a declaration at load — treat it as a given
here; its own completeness is a separate round's subject.

Both modules are importable, so a declaration and a value can be built
and run in a few lines. Use that.

## 2. Scope and the failure model

**What this round looks for: ACCIDENT and SLOPPINESS — a construct whose
documented meaning and implemented behaviour differ.** The declaration
shipped today exercises only some of what the language can express, so
the interesting cases are legal declarations NOBODY HAS WRITTEN YET: the
next chapter will write them, and a construct that means something other
than its documentation says will mislead its author.

Out of scope, recorded as CARRIED-SCOPE with no proposed fix: the
direction; whether the language is well designed; performance; naming
taste; the load-gate's completeness; anything about the deleted
predecessor implementation.

## 3. The lenses

**Lens A — construct by construct.** Work through `vocabulary.ts` and for
each construct ask: what does the doc comment claim, and does the engine
do that? Write a small legal declaration using it and run a value
through. The constructs, so none is skipped for lack of a list: the node
kinds (fixed and open maps, plain map, list, string, integer, enum,
union, raw, value-class reference, delegate); `presence` with its
`at`/`code`/folded-into-type-lane variants; `nonempty` and its `gating`
flag; `grammar`; `keyClass` with `keyLaneAt`; `deepKeyStringness`;
`keysSubsetOf`; `unique` with its `at` grain; `memberOf` and
`disjointFrom` with their `at` grain and `dependsOn`; `default`;
`removedKeys` and `removedValues`; `sourceForm` and `resolvedForm`;
`channel` at node and enum-member grain; `laneOrder`'s three values; the
equals cross rule's two directions and grains; the normalizer's two
hooks.

Report every disagreement between comment and behaviour, in either
direction — the comment may also be describing something the engine no
longer does.

**Lens B — boundary values.** For the constructs above, the values at the
edges: empty containers versus absent keys; a single-member union; an
enum with one member; a list of one; a grammar matching the empty string;
a `default` that is itself an empty container; a required field whose
value is legitimately `undefined`-looking. Does each behave as documented?

**Lens C — grain addressing on declarations nobody has written.** The
language lets a finding be addressed at a container or at a segment or an
index (`at`, `keyLaneAt`, `memberLaneAt`). Today's declaration uses some
combinations; the language permits more. Build the unused combinations
and check the finding's path is what the documentation says it is — a
path that is wrong is worse than a missing check, because a reader
follows it to the wrong place.

**Property-style probing is encouraged**: generate legal declarations and
values, assert what the specification says should happen, and report
disagreements. Say how you generated them so the result is reproducible.

## 4. Evidence bar and finding format

**Every finding must carry an executed command and its real output**, or
be marked `unrun`. Work under `/tmp`; do not modify the repository.

```
### F<n> — <one-line title>
CLASS: IN-SCOPE | CARRIED-SCOPE | UNRUN
LENS:  A | B | C
WHAT:  the disagreement in one sentence — what the doc says, what the engine does
WHERE: vocabulary.ts:<line> versus engine.ts:<line>
EVIDENCE:
  <exact command>
  <exact output>
WHY IT MATTERS: what the next declaration author would get wrong
FIX SKETCH: (IN-SCOPE only) the smallest correction, and say which side
            you would change — the comment or the code
```

That last point matters: when a comment and code disagree, either could
be the mistaken one. Say which you think it is and why.

## 5. Rules of engagement

- **Do not modify the repository.** A byte guard runs before and after.
- **Do not propose new machinery.**
- **Proportionality.** A correction larger than the thing it corrects is
  itself a finding.
- **Vocabulary.** Routine software quality review: a specification and
  its interpreter, checked against each other. Ordinary engineering terms
  throughout.
- **A clean result is a real result.** Do not manufacture findings; do
  not soften a real one.

## 6. Closing message

Only this:

- your tree's HEAD;
- counts: IN-SCOPE / CARRIED-SCOPE / UNRUN;
- roughly how many of the constructs in Lens A's list you actually
  exercised, and which you did not reach;
- one sentence: **can a declaration author trust the vocabulary's doc
  comments as a specification of what the engine will do?**

     4	 * This module is TYPES ONLY — it defines the shape a surface declaration

    17	/**
    18	 * A message template: literal text with `{placeholder}` slots the engine
    19	 * interpolates (vocabulary #18 — `message:`, the PARITY carrier; ADR-019
    20	 * D5 pre-names message wording as a delta class, and a literal template
    21	 * is the choice that keeps the measured wording byte-identical).
    22	 *
    23	 * The closed placeholder set, all supplied by the engine from the node's
    24	 * own evaluation context — never rule-specific:
    25	 *
    26	 * - `{path}`      the finding's own path
    27	 * - `{key}`       the offending key, raw
    28	 * - `{keyJson}`   the offending key, JSON-quoted
    29	 * - `{value}`     the offending value, DESCRIBED (`a map`/`a list`/literal)
    30	 * - `{valueRaw}`  the offending value, rendered bare (no quoting) — the
    31	 *                 form the measured messages that embed a value inside
    32	 *                 their own quotes need
    33	 * - `{valueJson}` the offending value, JSON-quoted
    34	 * - `{source}`    the offending value's raw source slice, JSON-quoted
    35	 * - `{grammar}`   the grammar's regular-expression source
    36	 * - `{members}`   the enum/allowlist members, comma-joined
    37	 * - `{keys}`      the node's legal keyset, comma-joined
    38	 * - `{label}`     the referencing site's label for a value class
    39	 * - `{ownerKey}`  the key of the nearest enclosing OPEN-map entry
    40	 */
    41	export type MessageTemplate = string;
    42	
    43	/**
    44	 * THREE path languages, named apart (design review F11: one type name over
    45	 * three interpreters promises a compatibility that does not exist). Each is
    46	 * checked at declaration load by `defineSurface`, so a path written in the
    47	 * wrong language is a LOUD error rather than a silent no-op.
    48	 */
    49	
    50	/**
    51	 * A SELECTOR path — an address into the value graph, read by the engine's
    52	 * selector interpreter.
    53	 *
    54	 * Grammar: a head of `$` (the document root) or `^` (the citing node's own
    55	 * container), then dot-separated segments, where the segment `*` means
    56	 * "every entry of an open map". Nothing else is legal: there is no `..`
    57	 * form, because `..` is indistinguishable from an empty dotted segment.
    58	 */
    59	export type SelectorPath = string;
    60	
    61	/**
    62	 * A FINDING-PATH template — where a cross rule's finding is addressed. It
    63	 * is NOT walked: it is rendered, so it carries literal path text plus the
    64	 * same `{placeholder}` slots a `MessageTemplate` carries (`roles`,
    65	 * `roles.{valueRaw}`). Selector syntax is illegal here.
    66	 */
    67	export type FindingPathTemplate = string;
    68	
    69	/**
    70	 * A NORMALIZER operand path — read by the normalizer's own resolver, which
    71	 * understands a `$` head, dot-separated segments, and the `.*.`
    72	 * open-map-wildcard separator. It is not the selector language.
    73	 */
    74	export type NormalizerPath = string;
    75	
    76	/** vocabulary #12 — the selector roots. `injected` is D8's ADMITTED
    77	 * widening: a selector root that is an injected SET rather than a
    78	 * document node (a set is a set; the relation and finding form are
    79	 * unchanged). */
    80	export type Selector =
    81	  | { readonly keysOf: SelectorPath }
    82	  | { readonly valuesOf: SelectorPath }
    83	  | { readonly collect: SelectorPath }
    84	  | { readonly union: readonly Selector[] }

    87	/**
    88	 * vocabulary #12 — a membership relation between the citing node's value

    90	 *
    91	 * `at` is the finding's PATH GRAIN and exists because the measured lanes

    94	 */
    95	export interface MembershipRule {

    97	  readonly target: Selector;

   100	  readonly at?: "self" | "container";

   107	/** vocabulary #11 — the duplicate lane and its path grain. */
   108	export interface UniqueRule {
   109	  readonly grain: "perOccurrence";
   110	  readonly at: "index" | "container";
   111	  readonly message: MessageTemplate;
   112	}
   113	
   114	/**
   115	 * The keyset lane ORDER. It is declared, not hard-coded, for the same
   116	 * reason `at:` is (audit §5 F3): the measured nodes DISAGREE, and an
   117	 * engine that picks one silently moves findings. Three measured orders:
   118	 *
   119	 * - `missingThenUnknown`             — the root keyset
   120	 * - `unknownThenPerKey`              — ref, round, activation, the
   121	 *                                      runtimeContext spec, every gate
   122	 *                                      config (the DEFAULT)
   123	 * - `unknownThenMissingThenValues`   — the step keyset
   124	 */
   125	export type LaneOrder =
   126	  | "missingThenUnknown"
   127	  | "unknownThenPerKey"
   128	  | "unknownThenMissingThenValues";
   129	
   130	/** Every node carries its declaration TAG (the contract's pointer target,
   131	 * D4) and the ratified rows it realizes (the tag-closure check's other
   132	 * half). `channel` marks a channel-SCOPED node: `file` for a
   133	 * SOURCE-BEARING attribute set (the engine runs it where a source exists
   134	 * and skips it where none does), `direct` for a key that exists only on
   135	 * the direct-construction channel because it belongs to the ADMITTED form
   136	 * rather than the authored one. This is D1's structural channel symmetry
   137	 * — the retired hand-partitioned "realization split".
   138	 *
   139	 * WHERE IT IS HONOURED, exactly (design review F7): on a FIELD of a
   140	 * `map.fixed`, where it scopes the whole field — legality, presence and
   141	 * evaluation. Nowhere else. A `channel` on a root, a list member, an
   142	 * open-map entry, a union case or a value-class definition is REFUSED at
   143	 * declaration load rather than accepted and ignored. (`EnumDecl` members
   144	 * carry their own member-grain `channel`, and `deepKeyStringness` carries
   145	 * its own; both are separate attributes with their own readers.) */
   146	interface NodeBase {
   147	  readonly tag: string;
   148	  readonly rows: readonly string[];
   149	  readonly channel?: "both" | "file" | "direct";
   150	  /** vocabulary #9 — materialized ONCE at admission. Plain `default:` is
   151	   * declarable and stays schema-side (ADR-019 D3); DERIVATION is the
   152	   * normalizer's. */
   153	  readonly default?: unknown;
   154	  /** vocabulary #13 — this node's failure suppresses its dependents. */
   155	  readonly gating?: boolean;
   156	  /**
   157	   * vocabulary #2 — the keyset membership obligation, declared on the
   158	   * FIELD rather than on its container, because the measured lanes
   159	   * disagree on both grains: the template's fixed maps report a missing
   160	   * key at the CONTAINER with one shared wording, every delegated config
   161	   * schema reports it at the KEY with its own wording, and one node (a
   162	   * gate binding's `uses`) folds absence into its own type lane.
   163	   */
   164	  readonly presence?: {
   165	    readonly required: true;
   166	    readonly at?: "container" | "self";
   167	    readonly message?: MessageTemplate;
   168	    readonly code?: string;
   169	    /** Absence is reported by this node's OWN type lane, not by a
   170	     * separate missing lane (a gate binding's `uses`). */
   171	    readonly foldedIntoTypeLane?: true;
   172	  };
   173	}

   175	/** vocabulary #1 `map.fixed` — a closed keyset. The legal keyset IS
   176	 * `Object.keys(fields)`; each field declares its own `presence`. */
   177	export interface MapFixedDecl extends NodeBase {

   194	  /** vocabulary #3 on a map. `gating` is OPT-IN and the template surface
   195	   * does not use it: ch8-C21's suppression binds MISSING or WRONG-KIND
   196	   * containers only, so an empty map is a valid map of its kind and every
   197	   * rule selecting over its (empty) key set still runs. The attribute
   198	   * exists for a surface that ratifies the opposite; declaring it is what
   199	   * makes an empty container gating, never the engine's own choice. */
   200	  readonly nonempty?: { readonly message: MessageTemplate; readonly gating?: boolean };
   201	  /** vocabulary #5 — the open-map key class and the finding's path grain. */

   244	  /** The alias-free / anchor-free / tag-free / `^[1-9][0-9]*$` ladder over

   253	}
   254	
   255	/** vocabulary #8 — an allowlist. `store` is the authored↔stored token map
   256	 * (ch12-C1). */
   257	export interface EnumDecl extends NodeBase {
   258	  readonly kind: "enum";
   259	  /** A member may be channel-SCOPED: the same `channel` attribute the node
   260	   * grain carries, applied at MEMBER grain. It is a widening, not a new
   261	   * construct — the measured token domain genuinely differs by channel
   262	   * (ch12-C1: the FILE face is authored camelCase, the direct face is the
   263	   * stored token the domain type declares). */
   264	  readonly members: readonly {
   265	    readonly value: unknown;
   266	    readonly store?: string;
   267	    readonly channel?: "both" | "file" | "direct";
   268	  }[];
   269	  readonly message: MessageTemplate;
   270	  readonly code?: string;
   271	}
   272	
   273	/** vocabulary #1 `union` (+ #10 `removed` at VALUE grain). */
   274	export interface UnionDecl extends NodeBase {
   275	  readonly kind: "union";
   276	  readonly literals?: readonly string[];
   277	  readonly mapCase?: NodeDecl;
   278	  readonly removedValues?: Readonly<Record<string, MessageTemplate>>;
   279	  readonly message: MessageTemplate;
   280	}
   281	
   282	/** vocabulary #19 — uninterpreted pass-through. */
   283	export interface RawDecl extends NodeBase {
   284	  readonly kind: "raw";
   285	  /** A raw node may still assert a container kind (ch12-C3's `config`). */
   286	  readonly containerMessage?: MessageTemplate;
   287	}
   288	
   289	/** `map.plain + canonicalJsonSafe` — the agent-config value class
   290	 * (ch12-C7). A PLAIN string-keyed map (never a class instance, never a
   291	 * JS Map); a non-plain container SUPPRESSES the canonical lane. */
   292	export interface MapPlainDecl extends NodeBase {
   293	  readonly kind: "map.plain";
   294	  readonly containerMessage: MessageTemplate;
   295	  readonly canonicalJsonSafe: { readonly message: MessageTemplate };
   296	}
   297	
   298	/** vocabulary #15 — a reusable named value class, with the citing site's
   299	 * `{label}`. */
   300	export interface ValueClassRefDecl extends NodeBase {
   301	  readonly kind: "valueClass";
   302	  readonly valueClass: string;
   303	  readonly label?: string;
   304	}
   305	
   306	/** vocabulary #16 — hand-off to an injected registration's own
   307	 * declaration. */
   308	export interface DelegateDecl extends NodeBase {
   309	  readonly kind: "delegate";
   310	  readonly registry: "gateCatalog";
   311	  /** The sibling field whose value names the registration. */
   312	  readonly by: string;
   313	  /** The delegation contract's own belt: a registration that reports
   314	   * failure with ZERO findings must still block admission, or the binding
   315	   * would be admitted with no effective config. A property of the
   316	   * `delegate` construct, not of any one row (audit flag I3). */
   317	  readonly beltMessage: MessageTemplate;
   318	  /** vocabulary #13 — the resolution lane this hand-off presupposes. */
   319	  readonly dependsOn?: readonly string[];
   320	}
   321	
   322	export type NodeDecl =
   323	  | MapFixedDecl
   324	  | MapOpenDecl
   325	  | MapPlainDecl
   326	  | ListDecl
   327	  | StringDecl
   328	  | IntegerDecl
   329	  | EnumDecl
   330	  | UnionDecl
   331	  | RawDecl
   332	  | ValueClassRefDecl
   333	  | DelegateDecl;
   334	
   335	/**
   336	 * vocabulary #12's `equals` relation — a two-direction set equality with
   337	 * its own message per direction and its own path grain per direction
   338	 * (ch8-C16's measured form: the used-but-undeclared finding reports at
   339	 * the container, the declared-but-unused finding at the entry).
   340	 */
   341	export interface EqualsRuleDecl {
   342	  readonly tag: string;
   343	  readonly rows: readonly string[];
   344	  readonly relation: "equals";
   345	  readonly left: Selector;
   346	  readonly right: Selector;
   347	  readonly missingFromLeft: { readonly at: FindingPathTemplate; readonly message: MessageTemplate };
   348	  readonly missingFromRight: { readonly at: FindingPathTemplate; readonly message: MessageTemplate };
   349	  readonly dependsOn?: readonly string[];
   350	}
   351	
   352	/**
   353	 * D3 — the NORMALIZER's declared hooks. A declaration says what is legal;
   354	 * it does not compute a value, so derivation is named here and realized in
   355	 * `normalizer.ts`, never in the validator. Each hook is a named capability
   356	 * with its ratified rows; adding one is D7 format growth, not an edit.
   357	 */
   358	export type NormalizerHookDecl =
   359	  | {
   360	      readonly tag: string;
   361	      readonly rows: readonly string[];
   362	      readonly hook: "expandAdvancesRound";
   363	      /** The open map of entries the flag map is expanded per. */
   364	      readonly over: NormalizerPath;
   365	      /** The entry's edge map, relative to the entry. */
   366	      readonly edges: string;
   367	      /** The declared advancing set. */
   368	      readonly advanceSet: NormalizerPath;
   369	      /** The produced sibling field. */
   370	      readonly into: string;
   371	    }
   372	  | {
   373	      readonly tag: string;
   374	      readonly rows: readonly string[];
   375	      readonly hook: "materializeEffectiveConfigs";
   376	      /** The open map of pipelines whose members carry a delegated config. */
   377	      readonly over: NormalizerPath;
   378	      /** The binding fields carried into the admitted form verbatim. */
   379	      readonly carry: readonly string[];
   380	      /** The produced field the effective config lands in. */
   381	      readonly into: string;
   382	    };
   383	
   384	/**
   385	 * The SUBSTRATE block (audit §2.3): how bytes become a value graph. Its
   386	 * obligations are declared here as data; the yaml library realizes them,
   387	 * which is why their WORDING is residual R6 (library-owned) rather than
   388	 * engine parity.
   389	 */
   390	export interface SubstrateDecl {
   391	  /** ch8-C6: the read stage's decode wording. */
   392	  readonly read: SubstrateBranch & { readonly message: MessageTemplate };
   393	  readonly parse: {
   394	    /** ch8-C34: the only accepted `%YAML` directive version, and the
   395	     * finding a different one produces (`{key}` is the authored one). */
   396	    readonly directive: SubstrateBranch & {
   397	      readonly only: string;
   398	      readonly message: MessageTemplate;
   399	    };
   400	    /** ch8-C4: the duplicate-key wording. */
   401	    readonly duplicateKeys: SubstrateBranch & { readonly message: MessageTemplate };
   402	  };
   403	  /** ch8-C5: the resolved graph must be acyclic, and the finding it
   404	   * yields at the cycle's path. */
   405	  readonly resolve: { readonly graph: SubstrateBranch & { readonly message: MessageTemplate } };
   406	  /** ch8-C23: the CLOSED issue-code namespace. Every `code` any node in
   407	   * the surface declares must be a member — checked at declaration load. */
   408	  readonly codes: SubstrateBranch & { readonly values: readonly string[] };
   409	  /** ch8-C22/C36: the every-stage belt's own finding. */
   410	  readonly internalFailure: SubstrateBranch & {
   411	    readonly path: string;
   412	    readonly message: MessageTemplate;
   413	  };
   414	}
   415	
   416	/**
   417	 * Every substrate branch is TAG-ADDRESSABLE, for the same reason every
   418	 * node is (design review F6): D4's contract form is "decisions plus
   419	 * declaration pointers", and a branch with no tag is an authority a
   420	 * ratified row can only cite by repeating its text — which is the one
   421	 * thing D4 forbids.
   422	 */
   423	export interface SubstrateBranch {
   424	  readonly tag: string;
   425	  readonly rows: readonly string[];
   426	}
   427	
   428	/** One declared surface: substrate + node tree + the named residual's
   429	 * declarable halves. */
   430	export interface SurfaceDecl {
   431	  readonly substrate: SubstrateDecl;
   432	  readonly root: NodeDecl;
   433	  readonly valueClasses: Readonly<Record<string, NodeDecl>>;
   434	  readonly crossRules: readonly EqualsRuleDecl[];
   435	  readonly normalizers: readonly NormalizerHookDecl[];
   436	}
